## Closed — August 12, 2026 (sales transcript review)

*13 real converted-athlete WhatsApp transcripts (Dec 2025 – Aug 2026) read in full. Produced `sales-playbook.md` — the pre-sale and onboarding message library — plus one decision, one duplicate found, and a lesson about this file.*

- [x] ~~**How far ahead is the plan published?**~~ — **DECIDED, Iván, Aug 12, 2026, and recorded in `methodology.md` §6.4:** week by week before the first test, four weeks loaded ahead after it. **The trigger is the test, not a date** — that's the point where zones are real and the athlete's actual week is known, so building ahead stops being work that gets reworked. *Opened and closed the same session; it existed as an item for about an hour.* **It was the only service complaint two different athletes raised unprompted, and they had been given opposite answers:** Roberto Palacios, Aug 11 — *"No me ha llegado el plan de la semana"*, three weeks past his test, looking at an empty calendar, promised 3–4 weeks ahead *"así no vuelve a pasar"*; Sergio, May 16 — *"¿Cuándo pondrás la rutina para las próximas semanas?"*, told the wait is deliberate so Monday's feedback shapes the week. Both answers were defensible, which is exactly why it survived: neither conversation felt like a mistake at the time.
- [x] ~~**Test-hygiene message inconsistency**~~ — **not a new item; a duplicate.** Already open as `athlete-onboarding-flow.md` §6 Stage 9 (*"lowest-hanging item since day one"*). Re-raised with fresh transcript evidence attached, which is the second-copy-that-drifts failure the hygiene rules exist to prevent. The evidence was appended to the original entry and the duplicate deleted. *Confirmed rather than suspected now: delivered three different ways in three conversations — "revisa las instrucciones del test" (Roberto P), two voice notes (Alfonso), a verbal explanation of ritmos (Christian).*

> ***The lesson worth keeping from this session, and it is about this file rather than about sales.*** *The review was written up as **13 open items, and cut to 1** the same day on Iván's push-back — he had spent the day removing items and this added more than he had closed. On inspection ten of the thirteen were never open loops. **Five were notes about habits that the new playbook already fixes in text** (the discount frame, the price menu, a wrong `{DEPORTES}` variable that shipped to a real buyer, the missing "what failed before" question, social proof sent as a greeting) — a habit to hold is a standing decision and belongs in the doc that owns the behaviour, not on a checklist. **One was the duplicate above. Four folded into items that already existed and needed a line, not an entry** — semana-ideal automation → Stage 9; the Loom video → the TSS/PMC explainer now in NEXT; the EN/PT library → the members-area translation pass; the All-Access→coaching downsell → the win-back sequence in the small slot.*
> ***A finding is not an open item.*** *Most of what a review produces is a decision to record or a line to append. Writing each finding as its own checkbox is how this file got to 50 KB in the first place, and the failure mode is not laziness — it is thoroughness pointed at the wrong artefact.*

## Closed — August 12, 2026 (small-items session)

*A deliberate low-stakes session: no branch work, five small items picked off the list. Two closed outright; three moved forward and stayed open with sharper triggers. Recorded here because the pattern repeated — **in four of the five, the item as written down was not quite the item that existed.***

- [x] ~~**Fix `automation/content-engine/SETUP.md`'s `tp-admin` invocation** — it still documents running with `CONTENT_DB_DSN` (a URI). It is now run with discrete `PG_*` variables, because the password contains `:` and `@` and the URI form is a live tripwire. (Promoted Aug 6, 2026)~~ — **done.** Corrected in **two** places, not the one the item named: the known `docker run` line in Step 4, and the Step 3 `--dry-run` block, which carried the same `postgres://` export and would have failed identically for anyone reading the runbook from the top. Each carries a dated inline note. Verified against source rather than assumed — `admin_service/app.py` prefers `CONTENT_DB_DSN` **only when set**, falling back to discrete `PG_*` keyword args, so the fix is to leave the DSN unset rather than to override it. Downstream copies in `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` and `triaperformance-project-instructions.md` corrected the same session. *General rule now recorded in the infra doc: a secret containing `:`, `@`, `/` or `?` cannot go into a connection URI unencoded, and hand-encoding it is the wrong fix — it puts a mangled secret into shell history.*
- [x] ~~**Explicit `Cache-Control` in `automation/Caddyfile`** — long-lived + `immutable` for `/assets/*` now that URLs are content-hashed, no-cache for HTML. Versioned URLs already make correctness independent of this. Trigger: any time.~~ — **done, but not as specified, and the specification was the interesting part.** "URLs are content-hashed" is true of css/js only. Counted against a clean build rather than assumed: `/assets/css` 755 of 755 refs carry `?v=`, `/assets/js` 334 of 334 — but `/assets/images` **0 of 1037** and `/assets/guias` **0 of 6**. A blanket `/assets/* immutable` rule would have pinned every image and all five lead-magnet PDFs for a year with no bust mechanism, so a revised guide would never reach anyone who had already downloaded it — and the weight-loss nutrition guide in LATER is a planned new one. Shipped as four tiers: `immutable` for css/js, 7 days for images, 1 hour for the guides, `private, no-store` for `/members/*` and `/admin/*`, `no-cache` for documents. *The document rule is the one that mattered: `?v=` versioning only works if the HTML carrying those URLs is fresh, so the two halves are one mechanism, and the August 6 stale-JS afternoon was only half-fixed until today.* Matchers are mutually exclusive by construction so their order cannot be broken by editing — deliberately unlike the members-area gates in the same file, which do depend on order.
  *Also cleared up in passing, so it isn't re-found as a bug: the local `_site/` contains 24 pages with unversioned css/js and a `/guias/index.html` with no source. Stale build output from Jul 29–Aug 2, before the `v` filter existed. `_site/` is gitignored and rebuilt on the VPS at deploy — **it cannot be used to reason about what is live**, and a fresh build shows 0 unversioned css/js refs and no `/guias/`.*

## Closed — the big branch: 1:1 athlete onboarding flow (opened Aug 8, 2026 · shipped Aug 9 · moved here Aug 10 when the next branch opened)

Home doc: `athlete-onboarding-flow.md`. Payment → classification → Twenty record → members access → welcome email (ES/EN/PT) → intake form → Postgres → Gemini briefing → email + Telegram, with no manual step in between. Technical record: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §12 addendum and §21.

**Closed August 9, 2026.** The exit path was the last gap: Private cancellation verified against a real email (the existing regex already matched — no change needed), and CoachMatch cancellation built from scratch, since it was never handled at all. Both tested live. Athletes are now churned, de-provisioned and sent a goodbye email automatically on either channel.

Two traps found in the CoachMatch emails, both recorded in `athlete-onboarding-flow.md` §5: TrainingPeaks' *"Cancellation **Request**"* subject **contains** its *"Cancellation"* subject, so a `contains` filter would churn a paying athlete mid-service; and the confirmed email carries **no email address** — `Nelson Carrion ()` — so that branch matches by normalized name and writes nothing unless exactly one record matches.

The live bug the branch uncovered, fixed the same day: **the subscription-lifecycle workflow tagged every 1:1 coaching sale as `ALL_ACCESS`.** `Filter - Is New Subscription` matched on subject alone, and All-Access and Private coaching share that subject. Fixed with an explicit two-allowlist `Classify Product` node and a `Route by Product` Switch whose fallback alerts and writes nothing; CoachMatch, previously dropped silently, got its own branch. Blast radius zero — no subscriptions of any kind since the workflow went live July 24.

The home doc was split the same day: `athlete-onboarding-flow.md` (17 KB, decisions and live state) + `athlete-onboarding-build-log.md` (CLOSED, the build record). It had reached 96 KB, ~60 KB of it instructions for building something already built — the same shape `open-loops.md` was in before its own split.

**What's left is in `athlete-onboarding-flow.md` §6** and is deliberately manual, except Stage 8 (persist the perfect week, and create `athlete_profile` with it). Not opened as a branch — see `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §21's closing note on why building a schema nothing writes to would be speculative.

Follow-ups this branch created that closed with it: `Error Collector (global)` set as the Error Workflow on **all** workflows (Aug 9) · the All-Access welcome email rewritten in ES/EN/PT (Aug 10), which also caught a placeholder `NOTE:` line rendering as the first line of the subscriber email — never delivered, since there had been no All-Access signup since July 24. *Lesson kept: a note-to-self inside a live template is indistinguishable from copy.* · the seven dead `Disponibilidad semanal` columns — decided Aug 9 to **hide, not delete**: nothing reads them (`e.namedValues` comes from the form submission, not the sheet), those 51 rows are the evidence base for the §3.5 redesign, and deleting columns on a Forms-linked sheet is the operation most likely to disturb the linkage.

### Session-log narrative moved out of `open-loops.md`'s header, September 2, 2026

***The twelve chained "Prior:" entries that had accumulated in that file's `Last updated` line — 43.8 KB.*** *Verbatim and in the order they were written, newest first, exactly as they appeared. Each is the session note of the day it names.* **Why they were there at all:** *the header convention is to lead with the newest session's note and push the previous one behind a* `Prior:` *marker — which appends forever and never sheds, so the first thing every session read was 44 KB of dated narrative before reaching a single open item.* ***The convention stays; what changes is that the chain now holds one entry and the rest live here.***

*Prior: September 1, 2026* — **the first Ahrefs Site Audit was acted on and closed in one session (small slot); three of its five exports were the same two problems seen from different angles and a fourth was not a problem at all.** *Two broken internal links, both authored, neither ever a deployed page:* `/calculadora-de-zonas/cycling/` *— the* ***English*** *path segment inside a Spanish URL, and `cycling` is a real segment on a real page, just under `/en/` — and* `/coaching/`, *which has never existed; the nav's Coaching entry is the homepage anchor* `/#coaching`. ***Both are the same mistake and it is the one worth naming: a URL reasoned about rather than looked up.*** **59 linked images with no alt text came from one line** *in the blog card partial all three listings share —* ***and that `alt=""` was not a defect***, *it is WCAG-correct for a decorative photo inside a link that already carries text; Ahrefs applies the stricter rule. Now the article headline, with the trade recorded in the partial so it is not reverted in six weeks.* ⚠️ ***The meta-description export was a change report, not an issue*** *— its single row is the Aug 27 `IRONMAN` → `IRONMAN U` correction arriving in a crawl five days later.* **A clean deploy shows up in that export and reads like a defect at a glance; remember this before the next crawl.** **The durable half:** `tests/internal-links.test.js`, *wired into* `npm test`. ***The writer agent invents internal links, and handing it a whitelist of real URLs is the wrong fix — that list is a product of the build and is stale the next time a slug changes.*** *So the build asserts it instead: every root-relative link resolves, every `<img>` has a non-empty alt.* **Verified against a fixture that fails, not only against a site that passes** *— §9's `gbp_keywords` lesson applied to a checker.* **488 pages, 0 broken targets, 0 alt-less images.** *Iván still has to commit, push and let the deploy cron run — nothing here is live yet.* *Prior: August 31, 2026 (second session)* — **the first monthly close is opened, and both findings from this morning's verification pass are fixed in the repo rather than carried into it.** *Sequencing was Iván's call and it is the right one:* **a close is the first consumer that quotes these numbers outside this repo, and a wrong number that has been published is a different problem from one that has not.** ***Close #1 ships with a full P&L*** *— Iván's decision, which makes the three-month cost pull from Mercury/Stripe/cards the one Month 0 blocker that gates Sept 1.* **All three Month 0 blockers are still open** *(cost inventory, `agreementDate`/`churnReason` in Twenty, canonical name spelling)*, and ***two of the three cannot be reconstructed later*** *— the Twenty fields capture only at the time, so every month without them is history that is simply gone.* **The runbook is also out of date in Iván's favour**: *it carries GSC as «Not today» and GBP as «a manual screenshot», and* ***all three of GSC, GA4 and GBP became queryable in the last 72 hours*** *— close #1 pulls them.* **On the two fixes:** *(1)* `gbp_keywords` *— both halves fixed, and the second half is the one already costing data:* **the keyword window was the daily window, and the two sources behave oppositely** *— GSC and the daily metrics* ***revise*** *recent days, GBP keywords are* ***published late***, *so a rolling 7-day window stopped reaching a month around the 8th and froze it. Likely explanation for the four months missing entirely.* ***The guard and the window turned out to be one fix:*** *re-reading a closed month would have been actively dangerous under the old write path.* **Shipped with a test that carries the pre-fix code as a fixture and asserts the old version fails** — *16 checks, no VPS, no Postgres, no network.* *(2)* **the GA4 internal-traffic rule is instrumented, not decided** — *the three options remain Iván's call; what changed is that a rule which has never matched no longer looks identical to a rule with nothing to match.* ⚠️ ***One correction that changes how option (a) must be done: the GA4 data filter must be `Testing`, not `Active` — an Active filter drops the events from the BigQuery export entirely, and an event that never lands can never be reclassified.*** ~~**Neither fix is closed; both close when Iván runs them on the box.**~~ **He ran them the same evening, and the verification produced two findings neither fix was looking for.** ✅ **`gbp_keywords` is CLOSED and moved to the archive** — *`ok`, 34 rows, six empty months named in `detail`, nothing kept because nothing had prior rows;* ***the same run under the old code would have deleted six months and logged the identical line.*** 🚨 ***But it disproved my own diagnosis, which is struck in place rather than deleted: the four «missing» months are genuinely empty at Google.*** *The widened window went back and asked. **The window fix bought not those months but the ability to know they are empty** — and the error shape is the Gemini alarm's, one day later:* **a mechanism proposed as «likeliest» before anything went and looked.** 🚨 ***The second finding is the more expensive one, and the new GA4 view produced it by being read for the first time: the property was created 2026-07-21, so «July» is ELEVEN DAYS.*** *External sessions read* **33 → 110** *as months and* **3.0 → 3.5** *per day —* ***both correct, one true.*** **That is §1(b) of the close runbook — a snapshot quoted as a rate — arriving five weeks early and through a different door, on the very first table close #1 would have read.** *Fixed in the view, not in a warning: `days_covered`, `partial_month`, `external_per_day`.* **Self-traffic is also worse than Aug 30 said: 59.3% of July and 40.2% of August were Iván's own**, *and `internal_pct` is a floor rather than a measurement — it is what four cookie-bound rules caught on a property where the one rule that survives a cookie reset has still never fired.* *Prior, same day (first session):* **the analytics pipeline's first unattended run is CONFIRMED and the GSC/GA4 item is CLOSED.** *Eight sources, all `ok`, zero errors, at 05:15 / 05:45 / 06:15, with the log files on disk carrying the same minutes — which is the check that distinguishes a script that ran and failed from a cron that never fired, since no row is written until Postgres is reached.* 🚨 **Two findings came out of a verification pass that was expected to close cleanly, and that is the argument for running the checks rather than reading them.** *(1) The GA4 internal-traffic rule* ***has never once matched an event*** *— `analytics_internal_devices` carries no `traffic_type` row at all, so self-traffic exclusion rests on two hand-added cookies and two narrow rules, and the incoming Bogotá tester on gym wifi is reachable by none of them. (2) `gbp_keywords`' write path* ***deletes a month unconditionally and re-inserts only when the fetch is non-empty***, *so an empty API response destroys stored data — the exact inverse of the GA4 upsert bug fixed the day before, in a script touched in the same session. Its zero rows today are, separately,* **real and unremarkable** *on a profile doing 60–96 views a month; do not re-raise that half.* ***Also worth carrying: the query this repo hands you for «did the cron run» cannot answer it.*** `analytics_pipeline_health` *returns each source's last run whenever it happened, so a cron dead since Saturday reads as a screen of green `ok` rows. Filter on time — and derive the expected source count rather than hardcoding it, since this session predicted five and the answer was eight.* *Prior, same day:* the Aug 26 drawer rebuild shipped one real bug; found on Iván's iPhone, fixed, and one unscoped selector was behind both symptoms he reported. See the August 31 note below. *Prior: August 30, 2026 (third session)* — **the GSC/GA4 pipeline is LIVE and verified on the real box, and the analytics warehouse now holds Search Console and GA4 alongside the pixel data.** 🚨 ***A claim made earlier in this same session — that the content-engine feedback agent's trigger was therefore satisfied — was RETRACTED before the session ended, on Iván's challenge. The 16 months belong to the DOMAIN, most of it the HubSpot site; the blog has only existed since Aug 4, 2026 and the first five near-miss rows contain zero articles. Full retraction on that item in LATER.*** *Search Console returned history back to **2025-05-06** — ~16 months, on a Domain property only verified in July, because it had been collecting through the HubSpot era off the legacy DNS record.* ***The bulk export would have started at zero today; the API decision bought the whole archive.*** **Four defects surfaced during the handover and every one was found by running something, not by reading it** — *a repo path that does not exist on the box (`~/triaperformance-docs`; it is `~/.hermes/triaperformance-docs`), an illegal `\_` escape in a BigQuery string literal, two spellings of "unknown" coexisting in one country column, and a cron line scheduled on the same minute as the content-engine publish job with both `git pull`ing the same clone.* ***The pattern is the finding: the Postgres half was tested against a real Postgres and was clean first try; the BigQuery half was never parsed by BigQuery and broke on its first run.*** **A pass that verifies one half thoroughly and the other not at all reports as "verified" and is not.** *The first five near-miss rows then produced a design change on their own:* **one is a 30-word LLM prompt someone typed into Google**, *at position 6.2 — which the research agent would have read as a topic. A `word_count` column now sits on both near-miss views, deliberately not as a filter.* **Second half of the same session: the Business Profile chain closed end to end** — *approval located on `Triaperformance-GBP` (the two-orgs split turned out to be load-bearing: `Internal` OAuth needs a Workspace org, and the personal-account project has none), four APIs enabled, OAuth credential minted — **twice**, after the first set was pasted into a chat transcript and had to be revoked — and `sync_gbp_data.py` live on a 06:15 cron.* **GBP is a credibility surface, not a channel: 60–96 views/month, 2–3 website clicks, zero calls or direction requests in six months.** *The row that earns its place is reviews — 9 in July, 1 in March, zero every other month, all 46 replied — which turns the close's "velocity, not count" argument into a measured series.* ✅ **Post publishing confirmed available**, closing the Aug 17 item, ***and the check exposed a live public post selling "321 planes".*** *Logged as the reason the publisher emits no typed figures; the post itself is NOT being corrected — see the standing decision on the catalogue count.* *Prior, same day (second session):* — **Gemini spend given per-caller attribution, and the obvious hypothesis was wrong.** *Iván asked what drives the weekly spikes in the AI Studio usage chart.* **There is no AI Studio MCP and it would not have helped: five consumers share one API key, so Google attributes spend to the key and never to the caller.** `model_usage` built, applied and verified live — but ***the finding is that it instruments the part that is probably not the problem***: the research agent is the only weekly job in the content engine and its *smallest* consumer (~25k tokens, one call), the heaviest observed day is a **Saturday** driven by **translate**, and the whole engine spans ~8k–61k tokens/day. **The two consumers that could actually explain a weekly spike — the Monday 03:00 Hermes check-in and Iván's own Hermes sessions — do not go through `call_model`, and `docker logs` confirms Hermes emits no token accounting at all.** *One number still settles the whole question and it is Iván's: total tokens for Aug 22 off the Studio chart, against ~61k.* **Two things were found on the way, neither looked for:** a probe answering the word "ok" spent **1 output token and 108 thinking tokens** *(so an in/out-only table would have understated that call 13x — read `billed_output`)*, and **`pending_drafts`/`approved_unpublished` were frozen at their pre-`topic` column list**, which the review UI reads with `SELECT *` — ***a candidate root cause for the grey-rectangle bug logged this morning as closed.*** *Recurring review landed in the monthly close rather than a new weekly ritual; see NEXT.* *Prior, same day:* **the first large publish batch (20 articles) went through the rebuilt blog pipeline.** The `topic` rollout finally completed end to end: the two n8n edits from Aug 25 had never been made, so every new article was publishing without a topic and rendering as a grey rectangle. Files patched, `sync-topics.py` added and run (63 rows reconciled, files treated as authoritative). **The batch also produced the first hard evidence of the research agent's blind spot — three near-duplicates of already-published articles, one pair written on the same day** — now logged in NOW alongside the corpus-awareness fix, the merge work, and two pacing numbers (`TRANSLATE_LIMIT`, and the `LIMIT 2` publish drain that is live for the first time and will make the next batch appear over days rather than at once). *Prior: August 29, 2026* — **the GSC/GA4 analytics feedback loop built and handed over (small slot), and the instrumentation promoted out of a LATER bullet into its own item in NOW.** *Three initiatives were each waiting on the same missing thing and none of them owned it — the content-engine feedback agent, the monthly close, and (as of this session) the affiliate program. While it sat as a sub-block inside one LATER bullet, anyone reading LATER concluded all three were parked.* **The mechanism was an open design decision and it did not resolve the way the Aug 12 note assumed — not a hybrid, but two opposite answers, one per source.** *Search Console goes through the API for both backfill and nightly feed, because the BigQuery bulk export is verifiably forward-only and choosing it would forfeit 16 months of history.* ~~***on day one for every article already published***~~ ***Retracted Aug 30, 2026 — the history is the domain's, not the articles'. See the retraction on the feedback-agent item in LATER.*** *GA4 goes the other way and uses the BigQuery export already running — because the property and its export were created the* ***same day***, *so the export covers its entire life; there is no history to lose, no new credential, no new API, no new IAM grant, and it avoids the Data API’s silent low-volume thresholding.* **Two Aug-12 statements did not survive verification and are struck in place rather than deleted** — "both APIs expose ~16 months" (a GSC fact, not a shared one) and "GA4 Data API, same shape different endpoint" (the GA4 half needed no endpoint at all). **The one deliberate departure from `sync_pixel_data.py` is worth carrying forward as a rule:** *that script’s `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` is correct for pixel hits, which are immutable, and* ***wrong for Search Console, which revises the last several days after publishing them*** — `DO NOTHING` would freeze each day at its first incomplete version, with every row present and every number quietly low, and nothing to see in a log. *The idempotency pattern is not the thing to copy; the mutability of the source is what picks the conflict rule.* **Geography is now a first-class dimension in every table** *(from the Aug 29 affiliate scoping — which marketplace can be monetised at all depends on it)*, and two design decisions were made* ***unrepresentable rather than remembered***: *position is stored additively so that averaging an average is impossible, and a five-column `analytics_sync_log` exists so that a dead cron and a genuine zero cannot be confused — which is this pipeline’s most expensive possible failure.* **Nothing here is done: Iván has nine setup steps and one verification query, and the definition of done is what that query actually returns.** Full record: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §9. *Prior: August 27, 2026* — **the authority layer shipped (small slot): credentials, three About pages in ES/EN/PT, and the site's first `Person` structured data.** *Raised by Iván as a question — "I am a TrainingPeaks partner, they send me most of the Latin American leads, are we leveraging that enough on the website?" — and the grep answered no, with a sharper miss than "not enough": the word* ***partner appeared exactly once on the whole site, inside the blurb an athlete copy-pastes on `/referidos/`.*** *The only place the relationship did credibility work was a message Iván never sends himself. There was also no About page at all, and the nav's `Coaching` entry was a bare `/#coaching` anchor with no page behind it.* **The research reversed the risk calculus in his favour:** TrainingPeaks' own help centre calls the mark **"Level 2 Accredited"** (never *certified*), **explicitly licenses the badge for website use**, and calls Coach Match **"a limited directory of coaches"** — so the sanctioned wording is also the *stronger* wording, because a named accreditation is resolvable by a search engine and an invented title is not. ***For the stated goal, "partner" is not merely riskier than the truth — it is weaker.*** Iván's call: *partner* may appear in body prose, never in a title, meta description or JSON-LD field — **prose is retractable, an entity fact is not** — and that line is now enforced by a build check rather than remembered. Full record: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §36. **Two stale-claim corrections rode along**, both pre-existing and neither previously logged: `certificado IRONMAN` → `IRONMAN U` (the programme has concluded; content moved to ESCI) and the ES homepage's hand-typed `45 reseñas`, which had contradicted its owning doc for thirteen days. ***Second pass the same day:*** *Iván supplied the badge files and the Stryd answer, so the credential set is now complete and illustrated.* **The Stryd item opened and closed within the session** — the mark is **Certified Stryd Coach**, the course behind it is the **Stryd Power Coaching Course**, and both are recorded (the mark is what a reader recognises; the course is what he completed). *He is listed at `stryd.com/coaches`, but that page opens a sidebar with no per-coach URL, so* ***no Stryd entry went into `sameAs`*** *— sameAs must identify the person, and a shared directory page identifies every coach on it.* **Badges added** for TrainingPeaks Level 2 and Stryd. *TrainingPeaks ships two variants and* ***the black-wordmark one is the only correct choice here*** *— every surface it renders on is white or Blue Wash, and the white wordmark would vanish, leaving an unattributed shield.* ***Two CSS bugs found by screenshot and invisible in the source, which is §23's lesson for the third time in this file:*** *badges at 40px rendered the TrainingPeaks wordmark as an illegible smudge (now 56px, and the strip is a 3-column grid so five credentials read as 3+2 rather than four plus an orphan); and on the About page* `grid-row: 1 / -1` *on the badge spanned only the single* ***explicit*** *row, so the issuer and note flowed back into column 1 and rendered on top of the image.* ***Third pass, same day — the strip was rebuilt after Iván saw it on the real homepage.*** *It rendered each credential as a name plus its explanatory note in a 3-column grid, which under the actual photo hero was a* ***~450px block of body copy with two indented rows that aligned with nothing.*** *Now one wrapping row, **names only**, 226px desktop / 292px mobile, verified at 1440/1100/390.* **The lesson is new and is logged in §36:** *both earlier screenshots rendered the hero as flat colour, because the hero image is set in CSS and the local server had no `assets/images/` — so the strip was judged against a dark rectangle instead of a photograph, passed every check, and was still wrong.* ***A component verified in isolation is not a component verified in place; stage the page's real assets, not only the files you changed.*** *One thing was deliberately NOT done: the fifth mark wraps to a second line and could be made to fit by trimming the repeated "Coach"/"Certified" from the labels.* **Rejected — these are issuer-granted marks, and shortening one to win a line break is the same error as inventing "partner", only smaller.** ***Fourth pass, same day — the strip became a logo band and moved down the page.*** *Iván: the position under the hero was itself unnatural, which is a different objection from the previous one — addendum 2 fixed the block's weight, this fixes its place.* **Now after the testimonials, before the final CTA: athlete proof, institutional proof, ask.** *Four logo tiles grouped by* ***issuing body*** *rather than by credential, because TrainingPeaks issued two of the five and showing its mark twice reads as a mistake. New copy carries Iván's stated* **1,500 TrainingPeaks-referred athletes spoken with** *— logged with the warning that the verb is load-bearing:* ***this is leads spoken with, not athletes coached***, *and the same site says '+100 atletas entrenados'.* **Two new open items below.** ***Fifth pass — band captions now print the full mark*** *(Iván: "it might be repetitive with the image, but we are also looking for crawlers" — right, and a logo asserts nothing to a crawler).* **The `capt` field was deleted rather than rewritten:** *once the caption is the full mark it is a second copy of `name`, which is the exact thing the one-owner rule exists to stop. Captions derive from `name`; `captSuffix` carries only what is not part of the mark, and is asserted never to reach the schema.* *Prior: August 26, 2026* — mobile nav drawer rebuilt (small slot), and two structural bugs found underneath a cosmetic complaint. See the August 26 note below. *Prior: August 25, 2026* — blog listing rebuilt as a filterable card grid, a closed `topic` vocabulary introduced and backfilled across all 60 articles, and a database/repo drift bug found and instrumented. One new 🚨 finding, four mechanical stale-figure fixes applied, one stale open-item count corrected.** *Full pass: `kb-hygiene-prompt.md`'s five steps run in full. Catalogue re-derived from `data/training_plans_inventory.csv` (328, ES 164 / EN 111 / PT 53) — confirmed clean, matches every already-corrected doc; live-URL cross-check against `/planes/`, `/en/plans/`, `/pt/planos/` could not complete this session (WebFetch hit a fetch-permission timeout, not a repo issue) — re-check next pass. Duplication script found no doc pair over the retirement threshold — nothing qualifies for retirement this week. Repo word count: 180,865 (no prior figure exists anywhere in the repo to compare against — recorded here as this week's baseline).* **The real finding: `site/_data/library.json` sells "Cyclist Core" as a live, gated tool on the EN and PT All-Access pages — fully translated marketing copy, `gated: true` — and no EN/PT version of the tool exists anywhere in `site/members/`.** It shipped ES-only Aug 18, 2026, three days before this file closed the members-i18n branch on Aug 21 claiming full three-language tool parity, and it was never logged here or given a home doc. New item below, flagged for a decision — not fixed, since fixing it means either building two tools or pulling live marketing copy, both Iván's call. **Three mechanical fixes applied, all stale numbers no other doc had caught:** `zones-calculator-brief.md`'s bare "303" (→ 328); `lead-magnet-sesiones-por-zona.md`'s "not yet wired" line, which contradicted `zone-magnet-runbook.md` and this file's own Aug 14 closure of that exact question, both carrying the same `verified: 2026-08-14` date; and `social-proof-and-reviews.md`'s EN/PT catalog counts (108/29 → 111/53), pre-Aug-12 figures nobody had touched since Aug 8. **WIP small slot, said out loud per the standing rule:** 11 unchecked items today, against the last honest count of 9 on Aug 12 — up 2, and the header note hasn't been revised in 12 days. Iván's call, same three options as every previous session has offered.*

**Second half of August 17:** the last open item from the Aug 14 status-claim audit closed — and it closed as a **false alarm**. The 🚨 "live page served a stale copy of itself" finding was **not** Iván's infrastructure: his `curl` returned `cache-control: no-cache` with no `age`/`via`/`x-cache`, so Caddy and the Caddyfile deploy path are both working correctly. *The stale page came from a per-URL cache inside Claude's own fetch tool.* **The pass built on "verify against a different source" filed its loudest finding by trusting its own instrument** — kept in full below, with the rule it produced (*when a live check disagrees with the repo, suspect the check first*), now item 10 in `kb-hygiene-prompt.md`. **Nothing from that audit is open any more.**

**Third session, August 17 — Gemini model migration, no branch work.** *Hermes and the content engine's research agent moved from `gemini-3.5-flash` to `gemini-3.7-flash` in the repo (GA rather than `-preview`, two generations newer, half the input and 42% of the output cost). **The writer and the n8n athlete-intake briefing were deliberately NOT moved** — every 3.7 benchmark Google published is coding and agentic, none of it measures Spanish prose in a brand voice, and that gets settled by drafting the same idea both ways rather than by reading a benchmark. ***Both deployed and verified live the same day*** — the research agent's default is live (no `CONTENT_MODEL` override exists), and Hermes was confirmed by a real Telegram exchange in which it searched the repo, ran Python and answered. **That verification message is what earned the session its real finding**, and it is not a Gemini finding: asked the plan count, Hermes read the CSV correctly and then led with a stale 303 from an uncorrected present-tense line in `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §17 — *and then wrote it to its own memory.* Source line fixed; the memory is open in NOW.  Five items picked off: **two closed** (the `SETUP.md` `tp-admin` tripwire, and explicit `Cache-Control` in `automation/Caddyfile`), both moved to `open-loops-archive.md`; **three advanced but left open** with sharper triggers — the coaching-token insert (repo side fixed, n8n side is Iván's), the box-wide runtime-path audit (now a script, `automation/audit-runtime-paths.sh`, not yet run), and `content-engine-brief.md` §9 (items 4/5/6 finally struck, four days after being flagged as needing it). **One new item opened** (in the promoted-from-infra list, not the slot): whether the `v` cache-bust filter should fail loud now that `immutable` makes an unversioned asset a year-long problem. *Waiting on Iván: two n8n node edits, one audit run, four `curl` header checks after the next deploy, one decision on the `v` filter.*

**September 1, 2026 — the first Ahrefs crawl acted on, and closed the same session (small slot).** Five exports, **two real problems**: two broken internal links and 59 linked images without alt text. Fixed in `site/blog/metodo-noruego-ciclismo-entrenamiento-umbral.njk`, `site/blog/mito-ftp-estatico-ciclismo.njk`, `site/blog/plan-entrenamiento-maraton-lesionado.njk` and `site/_includes/partials/post-card.njk`. Full record: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §37. **Three things worth carrying forward.** *(1)* ***Both broken links were plausible rather than random***, *which is why nothing caught them: `cycling` is a real path segment (under `/en/`) and `/coaching/` is a page this site plainly ought to have. A reviewer reading the sentence would not stop on either.* *(2)* **The alt-text finding was a false positive that was still worth acting on** — `alt=""` on a decorative image inside a link with its own text is the WCAG-correct answer, and Ahrefs flags it anyway. ***Distinguishing "the tool is wrong" from "do nothing" is the judgement here***, *and the reasoning went into the partial's own comment block rather than into this file, because the person tempted to revert it will be reading the partial.* *(3)* ***The fix that matters is the test, not the three links.*** *The links were a symptom of an agent that writes URLs without being able to check them, and the check cannot live in the prompt — only the build knows what exists.* **Open, and it is Iván's:** *commit, push, let the deploy cron pull; then re-run the Ahrefs audit to confirm the two 404s and the 59 images clear.*

**August 31, 2026 — the drawer rebuild's own bug, found on a real iPhone (small slot).** Iván: *"if I click Planes, the sub menu opens, if I click it again it does not close. Same with Coaching"* — plus, in a narrowed Chrome window, groups opening on hover and shoving the next item out from under the cursor. **Two reports, one unscoped line of CSS**: `.nav-dropdown:hover .nav-dropdown-menu { display: block }` was written for the desktop dropdown and never gated to it. On iOS a tap latches a *sticky* `:hover`, and that latched rule at specificity (0,3,0) outranked the drawer's `display: none` at (0,1,2) — ***so the second tap did remove `.open` and did set `aria-expanded="false"`, and was simply outvoted. The handler was never broken.*** Fixed by gating hover-open on `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` **and** `min-width: 861px`, with tap-to-open covering everything that gate excludes. Full record: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §35 addendum. **Three things worth carrying forward.** *(1)* **When a behaviour is split across CSS and JS, the two conditions have to be the same condition.** The bug was exactly a JS handler that stopped at 861px against a CSS rule that did not — and the obvious fix, a stronger selector, would have left the narrow-desktop symptom completely untouched. Reading both reports as one bug is what produced the right fix. *(2)* ***The Aug 26 session verified this component in a real headless browser, across five viewports, and still missed it — because every check was a mouse check.*** Touch was never emulated, so the one interaction mode the bug lived in was the one never exercised. The suite now runs iPhone, narrow-mouse, desktop and iPad-landscape — the last covering a case neither report mentioned: wide *and* touch, where a width-only gate would have made `menuOnly` "Recursos" a dead label. *(3)* ***A failing test was wrong, not the code*** — "1st tap on Coaching navigates" failed because `nav.json` had changed underneath it on Aug 27 (§36 gave Coaching a dropdown), so the assertion described a nav that no longer existed. *A stale assertion fails identically to a regression.* **One invariant recorded in `nav.json` as a result:** a dropdown parent with a `url` of its own must repeat that url as one of its children, because the parent row does not navigate on mobile. All three languages satisfy it today — check it whenever a nav item gains children.

**August 26, 2026 — mobile nav drawer rebuilt (small slot; the referral branch is untouched).** Raised by Iván from a screenshot of the open drawer: *"I don't like how it looks."* Measured first, in a real headless browser: **1368px of content in an 844px viewport**, because both dropdowns were permanently expanded and every one of the 17 rows was 17px with a 20px margin — *Contacto* and the language switcher were both below the fold of a panel that gave no sign it scrolled. Fixed with collapsed accordion groups, a type scale that distinguishes a heading from its own children, padding-based 44px rows, and a near-full-width panel. Full record: `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §35. **Three things are worth carrying forward, and only the first is about the menu.** *(1)* **Two of the four causes were not cosmetic.** `header nav` was never `display: flex` — it has carried `flex-direction: column` and `align-items: flex-start` for months, both inert, and the bug stayed invisible because block layout produced the same picture the flex properties were asking for. And the overlay could never dim the header row: it sat outside `.site-nav-sticky`, and **`position: sticky` always creates a stacking context regardless of `z-index`**, so no z-index would have fixed it — the fix was moving one line of markup inside. *(2)* **The drawer had no close button.** The hamburger is a non-positioned child of the same stacking context, so the open panel painted over it; the only exit was a 70px strip of overlay. That is not a styling defect, and it survived every previous look at this component because the person looking already knew where the strip was. *(3)* **`members.css` was keeping its own 260px copy of the same drawer at a different breakpoint** — deleted, members pages now inherit the one component. *This project's one-owner-per-figure rule applied to CSS: the copy was three properties adrift and nobody had noticed.* ***The lesson is §23's, again, in a new place: a rule present in the source is not a rule that applies. Only the computed value — or in this case, a `margin-top: auto` that visibly did nothing — discriminates.***

**August 25, 2026 — blog listing rebuilt, and a database/repo drift bug found.** The listing went from a single column of text teasers to a filterable three-up card grid with a lead card and topic pills, in all three languages. Three things are worth carrying forward. **(1) The half-width layout was one line** — `.post-teaser { max-width: 65ch }`, a *reading* measure applied to a *listing*; correct on article prose, wrong on something that is scanned rather than read. **(2) `category` could never have driven the pills**, which is why a closed `topic` vocabulary now exists: the writer had invented **35 distinct category values across 60 articles** in three languages. `category` stays as the article page's fine-grained label; `topic` is the structural key, backfilled by `transKey` group so siblings cannot disagree. A 9th topic, `weight-loss`, was added against a tidier 8-topic taxonomy on commercial grounds — those plans are roughly half of recent sales and would otherwise be buried under running and cycling. **(3) Stock photography is now permitted on blog cards only**, amending `brand-guidelines.md` §6 rather than deleting the rule; AI-generated athletes stay prohibited. Cards render a topic-coloured panel when no image exists, so the grid was finished on day one and photos are an upgrade rather than a dependency.
***The bug is the more valuable finding, and it is this project's own hygiene rule applied to code: one owner per fact.*** *Piece 12 was published correctly by n8n on Aug 6, then **deleted from the repo on Aug 20 in a commit about the athlete flow** — swept up in unrelated work. Nothing told the database. It kept reporting `PUBLISHED`, and on **Aug 24 `auto_translate`, which treats `PUBLISHED` as ground truth, spent two generations producing EN and PT siblings of an article that had been off the site for four days** — siblings that went live declaring an hreflang group with a hole in it. **The repo owns whether an article exists; the database owns whether it is meant to; nothing checked they agreed.** `notify.py` now walks every `PUBLISHED` piece's `file_path` and reports drift daily until it is resolved, alongside the failed-agent and stale-agent checks. Deliberately one-directional — files without a database row are the six hand-written articles and reporting them would be noise.*

**Third session, Aug 12 — no build, a regroup after 8 days away, plus scoping.** The content engine had run unattended the whole time with every cron `OK` and nothing broken; the backlog was purely Iván's two review gates. Items logged, none started: **GSC/GA4 instrumentation** for the feedback agent (LATER — including why an MCP connector is the wrong mechanism: it would give *Claude in a conversation* the data, not the cron agents that need it), **content engine V2** for reducing the two review gates (LATER — translation auto-approve first, because translation is the only stage with a ground truth to compare against), **distribution** as the unbuilt half (LATER), and two small verifications from the Aug 4 session's untested tail plus a five-minute GSC indexing check (small slot). *One observation of Iván's is doing real work in the V2 entry: the research agent predates the translator and still proposes EN/PT ideas for universal topics, which produces two English articles competing on the same topic.*

***The pattern worth carrying out of this session: in four of the five items, the item as written down was not the item that existed.*** *The `SETUP.md` fix was in two places, not one. The `Cache-Control` item's premise ("URLs are content-hashed") held for css/js and was false for images and the lead-magnet PDFs, so the prescribed blanket rule would have pinned all of them for a year. The prescribed `COALESCE(NULLIF(...))` would not have caught the value actually arriving. The audit script's own first draft reported `outside: 0` regardless of what it found. **None of that was visible from the item text; all of it came from checking the real artefact — a clean build, the schema, the source file, a fixture.** For small items specifically, the temptation is to implement the note rather than verify it, because the note is short and the item is "easy." That is exactly backwards.*

*Previous session, August 11, 2026 — no branch work.* One item added to LATER (weight-loss nutrition guide), and **NEXT #8 sharpened**: the B-lite call is now a *testing-and-zones consultation* on the top 20 sellers, and is logged as the trial run for the coach hire in LATER. Detail moved to `growth-roadmap.md` §Storefront decision 3, which also had a top-10 / top-sellers contradiction with its own Phase 3 — resolved to 20. *Previous session, August 10, 2026 (second session):* **new big branch opened: members area in three languages.** `/members/en/` and `/members/pt/` shipped as a skeleton behind the same single Caddy gate; login now routes by the token's `preferred_language`; an unvalidated `next` open-redirect was fixed on the way past; a ghost "Members Access" button added to the public nav in all three languages. No tools translated — that is the branch's definition of done, not its start. The closed 1:1 onboarding branch moved to `open-loops-archive.md`. *Earlier same day: All-Access welcome email rewritten in ES/EN/PT and tested live (plus a placeholder NOTE removed that had been rendering as the first line of the subscriber email), and four items closed on Iván's confirmation (D1 test record, both Gemini items, the EN Ironman flag check). Aug 9: 1:1 onboarding branch closed. Aug 8: branch opened, plus a knowledge-base consolidation + factual-correction session.* This file was 50 KB, roughly 87% closed items and session history; all of that now lives in **`open-loops-archive.md`**. This file is the live list and nothing else.

***STANDING DECISION, Iván, August 30, 2026 — the catalogue count is 328 and it is CLOSED. Stop re-deriving it and stop flagging discrepancies in existing copy.*** *Source of truth is the **TrainingPeaks marketplace profile**, which reads "Showing 328 plans" — not a re-count. `data/training_plans_inventory.csv` is a mirror; reconciling **which** 328 would mean checking plans one by one against the spreadsheet, and that work has no buyer.* **Why this rule exists:** *this number has been raised in session after session, for a different reason each time, and every raise spends Iván's attention on something* ***no customer is auditing***. *He can publish more or unpublish stale ones at any time; the figure moves and that is fine.* **What still applies:** *use 328 in NEW copy, because getting it right when writing costs nothing.* **What no longer applies:** *auditing live posts, pages or listings for a wrong count, or reporting one as a finding.* *(The `321 planes` GBP post found today is an example of what is now out of scope — logged as history, not as an open item.)*

**Catalog count, for anything that needs it: 328 published plans (ES 164 / EN 111 / PT 53) — and as of August 12, 2026 that is also the total number of inventory rows. Every row in `data/training_plans_inventory.csv` is now a published, live plan; there is no unpublished remainder to reason about.** *Worth protecting: that invariant is what makes "the catalogue" and "the file" the same thing, and it is the first time this week they have been.* Owner: `data/training_plans_inventory.csv`, `is_published=TRUE`. Do not hand-type it from memory — it has moved five times in six days. *(Aug 12, 2026: **303 → 327**, the largest single move yet — 24 plans confirmed live by Iván against TrainingPeaks and flipped. **Portuguese went 29 → 52, up 79%**, and is no longer the thin catalogue it has been described as all week. ES unchanged at 164. Aug 10, 2026: 301 → 303, the two EN full-distance Ironman plans.)*

Five items that lived only in `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` were promoted here in the same session, because that doc's "Open items" section has been retired — it duplicated this file by design and had started to contradict it.

This file replaces the open-item lists previously scattered across `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md`, `growth-roadmap.md` (Sequencing), `content-engine-brief.md` (Open decisions), and the runbooks. Those docs keep the *detail*; this file is the *list*. Update it at the end of every working session.

## Agent-invented internal links (NOW item + NEXT #14) — closed September 4, 2026

- [ ] 🆕 **`npm test` reports 6 broken internal links, all invented by the writer agent in blog articles.** *(Found September 4, 2026, running the suite after unrelated work — **not** caused by it; no blog file was touched.)*

  ```
  /en/zone-calculator/cycling/      ← /en/blog/double-threshold-mistake-amateur-cyclists/
  /en/zone-calculator/running/      ← /en/blog/intensity-regulation-adjusting-threshold-paces/
  /en/cycling-zone-calculator/      ← /en/blog/static-ftp-myth-durability/
  /en/coaching/                     ← /en/blog/load-adjustment-rules-marathon-injury/
  /calculadora-de-zonas/cycling/    ← /pt/blog/erro-duplo-limiar-ciclistas-amadores/ + /pt/blog/mito-ftp-estatico/
  /pt/calculadora-de-zonas/running/ ← /pt/blog/regulacao-intensidade-ajustar-ritmo-limiar-tempo-real/
  ```

  🚨 ***Every one is `ai-infrastructure-documentation.md` §37's finding repeating, and two are literally the same two URLs it fixed on September 1.*** *§37 fixed `/calculadora-de-zonas/cycling/` — the English path segment inside a Spanish URL — and `/coaching/`, which has never existed. **Both are back, in different articles, three days later.*** *The English calculator lives at `/en/training-zones-calculator/`, the Portuguese at `/pt/calculadora-de-zonas/`, and Coaching is the homepage anchor `/#coaching`, never a page.*

  **So the fix §37 shipped is working exactly as designed and is not enough.** *`tests/internal-links.test.js` catches these at build time, which is why they are visible at all — but it runs on demand, and* ***nothing stops the writer agent generating the same wrong URLs tomorrow.*** **The durable fix is upstream, in the writer's prompt or a post-generation link rewrite, not another round of hand-fixing seven articles.** *Note the failure is stable and cheap to prevent: the agent reaches for the URL a reader would guess, and there are only a handful of real ones.*

  ⚠️ **These are live pages with dead links in them today.** *Two jobs, and they should not be confused: fix the seven articles, then stop the source. The second one is the item.*

- [ ] **#14 · Seven broken internal links in agent-written blog articles.** *(Found September 4, 2026 by running `tests/internal-links.test.js` — the guard built in §37 for exactly this, doing its job.)* **All seven are links to zone-calculator and coaching URLs that do not exist**, invented by the writer agent inside article bodies:
  `/en/zone-calculator/cycling/`, `/en/zone-calculator/running/`, `/en/cycling-zone-calculator/`, `/en/coaching/`, `/calculadora-de-zonas/cycling/` (twice, from two PT articles) and `/pt/calculadora-de-zonas/running/`.
  ⚠️ ***The shape is the point: the agent is writing plausible URLs for pages it believes should exist, and four of the seven are near-misses on the real calculator slugs.*** *The real EN path is `/en/training-zones-calculator/`; there is no per-sport sub-path in any language.* **Two candidate fixes and they are not the same job:** (a) correct the seven links, which is ten minutes and fixes today; (b) give the writer the real internal URL set the way it is already given the plan catalogue, so it links from data instead of from memory. *(b) is the one that stops this recurring, and it is the same fix shape as the plan-card language check in §20.* **Nothing is redirecting these today — they 404.**

### CLOSED September 4, 2026 — agent-invented internal links, both items, plus the two classes the guard could not see

**Both items above are one item and they are closed together.** *The NOW entry and NEXT #14 are the same seven links found the same day, written up twice — which is itself the duplication this list keeps producing. Closed as one.*

**(a) The seven links: already fixed in commit `4774ed2`, before this session opened.** *Verified by reading the diff rather than the note: `/en/zone-calculator/cycling/` → `/en/training-zones-calculator/cycling/`, and so on across all seven articles.* ⚠️ ***One assertion in NEXT #14 was wrong and is worth keeping as a wrong belief: "there is no per-sport sub-path in any language."*** *There is. `/en/training-zones-calculator/{cycling,running,swimming}/`, `/calculadora-de-zonas/{ciclismo,running,natacion}/` and `/pt/calculadora-de-zonas/{ciclismo,corrida,natacao}/` have all existed since the zone-magnet build (Aug 13). The agent's four "near-misses" were nearer than the item credited — it had the right shape and the wrong slug, which is a different and easier failure to fix than inventing a structure.*

**(b) The root fix shipped: `site_links()` + `check_links()` in `writer_agent.py`.** *This is option (b) from the item — link from data, not from memory — and it is deliberately the same shape as planCard, which has never produced a broken link because inventing one is impossible.*
  • `site_links(lang)` derives the linkable URL set from `site/` **at run time**: a directory holding an `index.njk`/`index.md` is a page, which is Eleventy's own rule. **That answers the objection recorded in `tests/internal-links.test.js`** — *"giving it a whitelist would not help, because the URL set changes with the build."* ***A derived set cannot go stale; a listed one can. The objection was to the wrong mechanism, not to the idea.***
  • **`noindex` is resolved the way Eleventy resolves it — directory data files, then front matter.** *Not a detail: the entire `/members/` tree is gated by one line in `site/members/members.json`, so a front-matter-only check would have handed the writer 40 gated pages and called them public.*
  • `check_links()` runs inside `validate()`, so a draft with an invented URL is **rejected before it reaches review**, for both the writer and the translator paths.
  • **Replayed against all seven historical failures: 7/7 caught. Replayed against all 116 published articles: 0 false positives.**
  • The translate prompt gained the rule that made this recur across languages: ***internal links are not translated, they are replaced.*** *`/pt/calculadora-de-zonas/running/` is what translating a slug's words looks like when the real page is `corrida`.*

**(c) A hand audit of every article and page found two classes the build guard was structurally blind to — both live at the time.** *This is the part the item did not anticipate, and it is the more useful half.*
  1. **Absolute own-domain links.** `site/blog/dolor-tendon-de-aquiles.njk` linked `https://triaperformance.com/members/aquiles/` — **a public article pointing a non-subscriber at a gated page**, which is the gated_teaser rule broken in the one place it matters. The test skipped it because it starts with `https:`. *Fixed: the routine is named in bold and the All-Access link beside it is now root-relative.*
  2. **Dead fragments.** Four plan-category pages had a "Ver planes" button on `#5k` / `#sprint` / `#running` / `#4-semanas` — **anchors deleted in the July 30 catalog refactor and never repointed.** *The page returns 200 and the button does nothing, which is why no crawler and no test ever complained.* And the members nav has shipped an FAQ item pointing at `#faq` on the **EN and PT** pages since Aug 10, where no FAQ section existed. *Fixed: the catalog section now carries `id="catalogo"` and the four buttons point at it; the EN and PT FAQ sections were written from the Spanish one.* ⚠️ **The FAQ copy is adapted, not new policy — every answer describes how the product already works — but it is customer-facing text that has not had Iván's eye on it.**
  🚨 ***The lesson, and it generalises past links: a check scoped to what broke last time only ever catches what broke last time.*** *`internal-links.test.js` was written on Sept 1 against the two classes Ahrefs had just found, and it was still passing 4 days later with a gated page linked from a public article and five dead buttons on the site.* **Now four checks, not two.**

**(d) One stale copy corrected on the way past.** `MARKET_NOTES["pt"]` in `writer_agent.py` told the translator the Portuguese catalogue "only has marathon, 5k, 10k, 21k and FTP" and excludes "swimming, HYROX or weight loss". ***It is the same false belief `/pt/all-access/` carried until it was fixed earlier the same day*** — the live PT catalogue is 53 plans across running (28), cycling (9), triathlon (8), swimming (4) and duathlon (4). **The note was steering Brazilian readers away from 22 plans they can already run.**

***Still open, and deliberately not done here: nothing checks EXTERNAL links.*** *There are none in any article today — the writer has never emitted one — so there is no live problem, only an unguarded direction. Worth a rule the day the first one appears.*


### Third write-up of the same finding, from the NOW list

- [ ] ✅ **Six broken internal links in blog posts — FIXED September 4, 2026.** *Found by running `tests/internal-links.test.js`, which is exactly what §37 built it for.* All seven files were content-engine posts inventing URLs: `/en/zone-calculator/{cycling,running}/`, `/en/cycling-zone-calculator/`, `/en/coaching/`, and — in two **Portuguese** posts — `/calculadora-de-zonas/cycling/`, *a Spanish path with an English segment, which is §37's original bug reappearing verbatim in a third language.* Every replacement was verified against the build.
  - [ ] **The writer agent is still inventing internal links.** *The test catches them after publication; nothing stops them being written.* **`tests/internal-links.test.js` is not in the content engine's publish path** — it is a repo test run by hand. Worth wiring into the engine's own gate, or the same handful reappears next month.
  > ⚠️ *Note for whoever runs that test: it reads `_site` and takes no override, so it silently checks whatever build happens to be on disk. It reported four already-fixed links until `_site` was rebuilt. Build into `_site` before trusting it.*

**CLOSED September 4, 2026 — and this was the THIRD write-up of one finding.** *The same seven links were entered here, in NOW, and as NEXT #14, all on the same day. `open-loops.md` was split on September 2 for exactly this and the behaviour survived the split.* **All three are now in this file, together, as one item.**

- ✅ **"The writer agent is still inventing internal links"** — *fixed at the source, not by wiring the JS test into the publish path as this note proposed.* **`check_links()` runs inside `writer_agent.validate()`**, so an invented URL is rejected before a human ever reviews the draft — earlier than the build gate, and it covers the translator path too. See the main closing note below.
- ✅ **The `_site` staleness trap** — *this note was right, and it cost time again the same day: the test reported 4/4 green against a build predating five live dead anchors.* **`internal-links.test.js` now refuses to run when `_site/` is older than `site/`** rather than answering confidently about bytes nobody is serving. ⚠️ ***That is the general shape of the bug and it is worth carrying forward: a guard reading a stale artifact does not fail, it lies.***

## Closed September 5, 2026 — the pixel `plan_id` gap

- [x] ~~**36% of marketplace plan views (1,055 of 2,930) carry no `plan_id` — a collection gap, not free to fix.**~~
  **CLOSED September 5, 2026. It was not a collection gap and it was free to fix.** *Of the 1,055 rows, **zero** carried a
  `price` and **zero** carried a trainingpeaks.com referrer, against 99.0% and ~71% for rows with a real `plan_id`.
  The query string was not partially lost — it was absent, so the request hit the bare Cloud Run root and was never
  on a listing. **Nine IPs produced all 1,055; one sent 1,008 of them in 90 seconds on 2026-08-31**, the day before
  close #1 read the table. Four such floods exist (2025-11-24 ×2, 2026-03-14, 2026-08-31), three running one pinned
  `okhttp/3.14.9`.*

  🚨 ***The belief worth preserving, because it is what kept this closed for four days: the September 2 note
  declaring the GA4 pairing "disproved" was itself the error.*** *It read: "the GA4 half closed as a **reading** bug…
  This half is a **collection** gap… one was free to fix and this one is not." **Both were reading bugs.** The
  pairing made in close #1 was right, and the note retiring it was wrong — and because the note sounded like a
  finding rather than a guess, nobody re-tested it. **The classification was reached by elimination and never
  segmented; one `GROUP BY` would have settled it on day one.**

  **Second lesson, and the more general one:** *`plan_views_clean` tested whether a request would **admit** to being
  a bot (`user_agent LIKE '%bot%'`) and reported the answer as validity. A spoofed Chrome string defeats that
  entirely — and `security-posture.md` F8 had predicted it in writing, in a Tier 3 finding nobody acted on. The
  structural test — a plan view names a plan — was available all along and costs one predicate.*

  **Four further defects surfaced in the same view, and the fifth explains why all four survived: it had never been
  in the repo.** *Catalogue sweeps counted as demand (14.3% of August); two of three personal IPs never excluded
  despite `open-loops.md` #2 claiming otherwise; `~~` is case-sensitive so `AhrefsBot` and `SemrushBot` sailed past
  `%bot%`; and NULL IP/UA rows were silently dropped by three-valued logic. Definition now lives at
  `automation/analytics/schema_plan_views.sql`.*

  ***Corrected figures:*** **August 2026 marketplace plan views are `1,604`, not `2,930` — 83% high.**
  *Nov 2025 → 1,164 · Mar 2026 → 1,663 · Jun 2026 → 2,708. `monthly-close/2026-08.md` and its metrics CSV corrected
  the same day; `triaperformance-growth-roadmap.md`'s median-views and view→purchase figures struck pending
  re-derivation.*

  ***Still open, in `open-loops.md`:*** *the collection-side fix (folded into the `plan-tracker-bigquery` runtime
  item, and blocked on whether the deployed source is the repo copy), and `audit-runtime-paths.sh`'s blindness to
  database objects.*

## Closed — September 5, 2026 · contact-form language branch

- [x] ~~**The contact-form confirmation email only fires when the Twenty write succeeds — repeat prospects get silence.**~~ **FIXED, TESTED AND LIVE — September 5, 2026 (Iván: "tested and green").**
  > **Two dead ends, both silent, both traced through the live connection graph:** `Already exists? ✔` *→ Telegram → Respond, **no email**; and* `Create Person ✘ → Is phone error? ✘` *→ Telegram alert → Respond, **no email**.* `Send confirmation email` *hangs off* `Person Created`, *which only exists when Twenty accepts the write.*
  > ***The returning-prospect case is the damaging one:** the visitor sees the success message in the browser, Iván gets a Telegram, and the person receives nothing.* **A repeat contact is a warm lead getting worse service than a cold one.**
  > 🔑 ***The fix has a precedent in this repo and it is Iván's own.*** `zone-magnet-runbook.md`: *"Deliberate side effect: delivery no longer depends on Twenty. If Twenty is down or rejects the write, the athlete still gets the guide and the only thing lost is the CRM record, which is the right way round for a lead magnet."* **The contact form is wired the opposite way round.** *Proposal in* `contact-form-pipeline-runbook.md`; *needs Iván's call before rewiring.*
  > ✅ ***Diagnosis closed the same day, and one hypothesis is dead rather than merely unlikely.*** *The failed execution's* `Check Twenty for existing Person` *returned* `{"data":{"people":[]},"totalCount":0,"pageInfo":{…}}` — **a well-formed Twenty response, so the filter works and `neverError: true` is not masking a broken lookup.** *The feared version — every repeat contact silently taking the create path — does not exist. **Do not re-raise it.*** *The rejection is the documented name-collision case (third occurrence), and widening the check to include name is deliberately rejected: `Juan Pérez` is not a primary key, and a false-positive dedupe silently drops a real lead.*
  > ✅ **The language branch itself works — Iván, September 5, 2026: three-language emails send and the zones-PDF link resolves.** *What is left is the wiring decision above, not the copy.*
  > ✅ ***The rewire shipped.*** `Config → Build confirmation copy → Send confirmation email → Check Twenty for existing Person`, *with the email node on **On Error → Continue (using error output)** into a new* `Telegram alert - email failed` *that rejoins the Twenty path.* **Both silent dead ends are gone: a repeat contact and a Twenty rejection now both receive the email.** *Accepted trade, same one `zone-magnet-runbook.md` already made: a double submission produces two identical emails.*
  > ⚠️ ***Two failures during the rewire, both mine to avoid next time, both now rules in the runbook.***
  >   **(1) `$json` is positional.** *`Check Twenty for existing Person` read `{{ $json.TWENTY_BASE_URL }}`, which worked only because `Config` was its immediate upstream. Moving the email in front of it resolved the URL to `/rest/people`.* **A rewire audit must cover data dependencies, not just the connection graph — grep the export for `$json.` before proposing any reorder.** *Five other nodes use bare `$json` and were fine; all read their immediate upstream and none of those links moved.*
  >   **(2) The `=` prefix is a mode marker, not a value.** *The fix was then pasted with its leading `=` into a field already in Expression mode, storing `==​{{ … }}` and producing* `Invalid URL: =http://100.70.89.17:3000/rest/people`. ***The tell is diagnostic: the expression resolved correctly and only a stray `=` was wrong — that is always a doubled marker, never a bad expression.*** **Quote expressions for the UI without the `=`.**
  > *Detail, and the rewire spec: `contact-form-pipeline-runbook.md`.*

- [x] ~~**Wire the zones guide's three languages to their delivery.**~~ **DONE September 5, 2026 — the contact-form confirmation email now sends in ES, EN and PT with the matching PDF.** *Verified by Iván on real submissions from all three homepages.*
  > ✅ ***ES rebuilt and shipped, September 4, 2026.*** *8 pages, **158 KB** (was 2.3 MB), same filename and same path — so the two n8n workflows, the members guides page, the marathon article and the `Caddyfile` redirect all kept working with **zero downstream edits**. Built by `automation/build-zones-guide-pdf.js` + `automation/zones-guide-content.js`.*
